"You call that intimidating?!" Heinkel barked out a harsh laugh, wiping
a tear from his eye. "Fuckin' hell, kid, you sound like you're running a
daycare, not a prison camp! I've had hangovers worse than your hostage
conditions!"

Kenichi let out a booming chuckle, grinning over at the embarrassed
beastman. "I gotta say, Garfiel-kun, if that's your idea of being a bad
guy, I'd hate to see you try to be nice. You'd probably end up giving
them foot massages and reading them bedtime stories!"

"Garfiel-san is very considerate," Schult nodded sagely, completely
missing the sarcasm as he looked at the blonde teenager with genuine
respect. "Priscilla-sama only lets me take one nap a day."

"Schult, do not compare my divine generosity to the pathetic coddling of
this mutt," Priscilla scoffed behind her fan, though her eyes danced
with amusement at Garfiel's utter humiliation.

Even Ram, sitting in the back, let out a soft, fond sigh. "Truly, Garf
is hopelessly pathetic when he tries to act cruel. A child playing
dress-up as a demon."

"Stop it! All'a you!" Garfiel groaned, covering his face with both
hands. Beside him, Otto was trying---and failing---to muffle his own
laughter behind his hand.

*Emilia and Subaru gain tense faces as he changes his expression to a
hardened glare of resolve.*

"*This is our demand. Break the barrier surrounding this Sanctuary. The
only one allowed to undergo the trial to do that is you. If you can't
break it, we won't let any of the villagers leave. Of course, you can't
leave here yourself, either!"*

The laughter in the room quickly died down as the true weight of
Garfiel's ultimatum settled over them once more. The terms of the
hostages might have been comedic, but the reality of the burden placed
on Emilia was crushing.

"So that's what this was all about," Wilhelm observed grimly, his
seasoned eyes shifting to Roswaal. "The villagers weren't taken to be
kept safe. They were taken to create a scenario where Emilia-sama would
have no choice but to take the trial."

"And since Roswaal-sama conveniently injured himself attempting it..."
Frederica murmured, her demi-human eyes narrowing in understanding. "It
leaves Emilia-sama as the sole savior of the Sanctuary. It forces her to
act as the hero to the very people who distrust her."

"He orchestrated the entire thing," Julius concluded, his voice laced
with cold disgust. "A trial to break a Witch's barrier... To force a
candidate for the throne into a corner just to further his own political
agenda. Despicable."

Emilia stared at the screen, her earlier embarrassment forgotten as she
watched her on-screen self process the immense pressure suddenly dropped
onto her shoulders. She felt the warmth of Subaru's hand next to her,
and the comforting grip of his mother on her other side.

*The scene moves to show Subaru's expression moving to one of worry as
it shows a chapel that has armed guards standing outside its doors. With
shots playing of the inside, showing the rounded up villagers and their
children looking at even more armed demihumans standing over them with
anxious gazes.*

*The door creaks as it opens, letting the sunset light up the darkened
church.*

*Subaru entered as villagers looked to him with surprise and even
relief.*

"*Subaru-sama!" "You're safe!"*

Naoko let out a small, breathless gasp, her hands flying to cover her
mouth.

"Subaru-sama...?" Kenichi whispered, his eyes widening as he watched the
scene unfold.

*He let a confident smile show on his face as the villagers all stood up
and huddled in front of him. "I'm glad you're all okay." He said to
them.*

"*Are the others all right?" One of the villagers asked.*

"*Is the lord recovering from his injuries?" Another asks.*

"*We heard he got hurt trying to help us."*

"*We're truly relieved!"*

"The lord got hurt trying to help them?" Heinkel scoffed loudly, an ugly
sneer crossing his face. "Yeah, right. Help them right into a cage."

"Ignorance is a fragile bliss," Crusch noted softly, her eyes entirely
devoid of warmth as she looked at Roswaal in the back row. "To think
they worry so deeply for the man who orchestrated their confinement."

"It makes a guy sick," Garfiel spat, though whether he was angry at
Roswaal, the situation, or his own past actions, he didn't elaborate. He
just glared holes into the floorboards.

Naoko felt the tears breaching her eyelashes once more, but this time,
they weren't born of horror or despair. "Look at them, Kenichi," she
whispered, her voice trembling with an overwhelming swell of pride.
"Look at how they look at him..."

"I see it," Kenichi replied, his voice thick with emotion. He stared at
his son on the screen---the boy who had once shut himself in his room,
paralyzed by the expectations of the world, now standing tall before
dozens of people who looked at him as if he were their savior. "He's
really... he's a man now, Naoko."

*Subaru's smile was warm as he looked around at the crowd. "Okay,
everyone, listen up."*

"*As you know, we got rid of the bad guy who attacked the village."
Subaru's call for attention was met with awed gasps and wide smiles.
"The other group that evacuated is back at the village, waiting for all
of you to come home."*

*People's faces that were once warm with joy suddenly fell into
hesitance and disappointment.*

"*I guess you've all been informed that it's not easy to get out of
here." Subaru gave them all a look of comfort and encouragement. "We've
got someone who's going to undergo the trial to set you all free!"*

"*You mean..." One of the villagers tried to speak to this, but another
asked in his place.*

"*Are you going to do it?" She asked Subaru softly.*

The question hung in the air, both on the screen and within the theater.

Emilia flinched.

It was a small, almost imperceptible jerk of her shoulders, but to those
sitting close to her, it was as loud as a thunderclap. She lowered her
head, her silver hair falling like a curtain to hide her face from the
rest of the room. Her grip on Subaru's sleeping arm tightened just a
fraction.

Rem, sitting directly behind her, noticed immediately. The maid's blue
eyes softened with a mixture of sympathy and a harsh, undeniable truth.

They don't expect it to be Emilia-sama, Rem thought, her chest aching.
They look at Subaru-kun, and they see the only one who can save them.

"Emilia-sama..." Petra whispered from a few rows back, having caught the
half-elf's reaction.

"Why wouldn't they assume it's him?" Anastasia muttered, her sharp
merchant's intuition picking apart the scene effortlessly. "He's the one
who fought the cult. He's the one who came to check on 'em. In their
eyes, he's the hero of their story."

"But it wasn't his duty," Wilhelm interjected quietly, his wise eyes
fixed on the trembling form of the silver-haired girl in the front row.
"It was the duty of their lord, and the duty of the royal candidate they
sponsor. Yet, the burden falls to the boy."

"*No, if I could do it, I'd be more than happy to, but..." Subaru moved
to the side, bringing a hand to his hip in a reveal as the villagers all
looked away from him to the doors.*

"He really is trying his hardest to be a good wingman for her," Kenichi
whispered with a warm, approving smile, watching his son set the stage.
"Setting up the grand entrance so she doesn't have to do it alone."

*Emilia walked inside with a brave expression, ignoring all the winces
and slight murmurs that filled the chapel hall.*

The warm smile on Kenichi's face faltered. Beside him, Naoko's posture
stiffened as the collective flinch of the villagers echoed through the
theater's high-quality speakers.

"*I think you all know who this is." Subaru's voice rang out. "It's
Emilia, the royal selection candidate. She'll be undergoing the trial."*

*The murmurs increased in velocity, and people started looking at each
other openly now.*

In the theater, the half-elf in question shrank slightly in her seat.
Emilia's hands tightened around Subaru's sleeping arm, her amethyst eyes
dropping to her lap. Even though she knew they had eventually accepted
her, seeing that raw, unfiltered fear and hesitation directed at her
past self still stung like a fresh wound.

"Why are they looking at her like that?" Naoko asked, her voice hushed
but laced with genuine distress. She looked over at Emilia, seeing the
girl's downcast expression. "She came here to save them, didn't she? Why
are they wincing?"

"Because she's a half-elf with silver hair, Mother-san," Al answered
from the row behind them, his voice lacking its usual playful tilt. "In
this world, that's the equivalent of walking into a room wearing the
face of the devil."

Naoko gasped softly, her eyes widening as she looked from the screen
back to Emilia. Kenichi's jaw set tightly. They had heard passing
mentions of the Witch's appearance before, but witnessing the casual,
everyday prejudice their son's crush had to endure was entirely
different.

"They are terrified of her," Rem bit out, her hands curling into tight
fists on her lap. "Even after everything she and Subaru-kun have done
for Arlam village, the deeply rooted superstitions of the world are not
so easily washed away."

"I-It's okay, Rem," Emilia tried to placate her, though her voice
wavered. "It's perfectly normal for them to be scared. I... I understand
why."

"That doesn't make it right," Garfiel grunted from his seat, crossing
his arms and glaring at the villagers on screen. As someone who had once
judged her harshly in this very Sanctuary, the blonde brawler looked
highly uncomfortable. "She's standin' right there offerin' her life on
the line for 'em."

"*I-I'm sorry you had to wait so long. In the place of your domain lord,
Roswaal L. Mathers, I will undergo the trial." Emilia proclaimed herself
before the villagers with uncertain resolve, clear on her face. She
suddenly bowed her head deeply, her silver hair flying behind her. "I
would appreciate your support!"*

*Emilia's hands were shown, shaking on her legs as she kept her head
bowed.*

"Hah!" Priscilla's mocking bark cut through the heavy air of the
theater. She snapped her fan open, hiding her lips but doing nothing to
conceal the arrogant amusement in her crimson eyes. "A candidate for the
Dragon Throne bowing her head to filthy, ungrateful commoners? How
utterly pathetic. A ruler does not beg for the support of those beneath
her; she commands it. This is why you remain a half-wit playing
dress-up, half-devil."

"Hold your tongue, Priscilla," Crusch's voice was like cracking ice. She
didn't turn around, her amber eyes fixed on the bowing silver-haired
girl on screen. "A true ruler takes responsibility for the plight of her
people. The Margrave abandoned them to this prison. She is lowering her
head not out of weakness, but out of a desperate, earnest desire to bear
a burden she did not create. There is a distinct honor in that."

Wilhelm gave a slow, respectful nod of agreement from his seat. "A
knightly display, Emilia-sama."

Priscilla merely scoffed, waving her fan dismissively, though she didn't
push the issue further.

Emilia's face flushed red, and she brought her free hand up to hide her
eyes. "Ah... um... p-please don't look at that part," she stammered,
feeling entirely humiliated. To have her bravado stripped away, to have
the entire room see just how terrified she truly was in that moment...
it was mortifying.

"Emilia-sama..." Petra murmured, her young eyes welling up with tears as
she saw the sheer terror the girl had been trying to suppress.

"You were terrified," Naoko said softly.

Emilia squeezed her eyes shut. "I was. I'm sorry. I know I'm supposed to
be strong for them, but I was so scared. I didn't know what the trial
was, and I knew they didn't trust me, and... and my hands just wouldn't
stop shaking."

Suddenly, Emilia felt a warm, calloused hand gently pry her fingers away
from her face. She opened her eyes to see Naoko leaning over the
armrest, holding her hand with a maternal gentleness that made Emilia's
breath hitch.

"Why are you apologizing, dear?" Naoko asked, giving Emilia's hand an
affectionate squeeze.

"Because... because I look so weak," Emilia whispered, looking at her
shaking hands on the giant screen.

"Weak?" Kenichi leaned forward to look past his wife, locking eyes with
the half-elf. His goofy demeanor was completely gone, replaced by the
steadfast, comforting presence of a father. "Emilia-chan, do you know
what true courage is?"

Emilia blinked, slightly taken aback by the sudden shift in his tone.
"N-No... what is it?"

"It's not about being fearless," Kenichi said, pointing a thumb at the
screen. "A person who isn't scared to jump into danger isn't brave;
they're just reckless. True courage is being absolutely terrified. It's
having your hands shake, your heart pounding out of your chest, and
every instinct screaming at you to run away..." He smiled warmly at her.
"...And then doing it anyway."

Emilia's amethyst eyes widened, shining with unshed tears as Kenichi's
words washed over her.

"You knew they were afraid of you, and you were terrified of what was
coming," Naoko added, her voice thick with emotion. "But you still stood
up there, and you still bowed, and you still offered to suffer for them.
That's not weak at all. That makes you an incredibly strong girl."

"I..." Emilia choked on a sob, entirely unequipped to handle such pure,
unfiltered parental validation. She looked down at Subaru, who slept
peacefully in his seat, and then back up at his parents. Now she
understood exactly where he got his bottomless well of kindness from.

"Thank you," Emilia whispered, wiping a stray tear from her cheek.
"Thank you very much."

"Don't thank us for pointing out the obvious," Kenichi grinned, leaning
back into his seat with his hands behind his head. "Though, I gotta say,
Subaru definitely hit the jackpot with you. You're totally out of his
league."

"Honey!" Naoko swatted his shoulder playfully, though she was smiling.

"What? I'm just being honest! He's gotta put in the work if he wants to
keep up with a girl who's got that much guts!"

The heavy, melancholic atmosphere that had settled over the room lifted
considerably. Even Rem found a small, genuine smile gracing her lips as
she watched the exchange.

"*Let me ask you just one thing." One voice rang out from between the
villagers as an elder lady walked past people who were parting for her
to make way to Emilia.*

"*Chief..." One of them whispered.*

*The older lady stood with her arms behind her, hunched back, looking at
Emilia. "Why do this? You bowed your head to us the last time you saw
us, too."*

*Images played of Emilia trying to talk to villagers with a pleading
expression, but many were glancing at her with disgust and even fear.
Women peaking behind their shoulders at her with weary expressions, and
older men who were ignoring her openly, a man even held his broom over
his head as a threat for Emilia to go away, while his wife held their
child behind their house door.*

"Goodness..." Naoko gasped softly, her hands flying to cover her mouth.
"They're looking at her like she's a monster."

"To them, she might as well be, Naoko-san." Crusch spoke from the side,
her tone lacking malice but heavy with the harsh reality of their world.
"The prejudice against half-elves, particularly those with silver hair,
is a deeply ingrained rot in our society."

Emilia shrank into her seat, her silver hair falling forward as she
subconsciously tried to hide her face from the Natsuki parents. She had
always known how the world saw her, but having Subaru's parents---people
from a world without such hateful prejudices---watch her be treated like
vermin brought a new, suffocating wave of shame over her.

"That's just cruel," Kenichi frowned, his jovial demeanor completely
vanishing. He looked at the man raising the broom with genuine anger.
"She's just a girl trying to help them. How can adults act like that to
someone half their age?"

"Because fear makes fools of grown men, Old Guy," Felt scoffed, "I grew
up in the slums. I've seen that look a million times. It's the look
people give when they need a scapegoat for their miserable lives."

*Even a last shot of Petra looking out of her window at Emilia, who
stood all alone in the village.*

In the row behind the knights, a choked, wet gasp echoed.

Petra had folded completely into herself. Her small hands gripped her
maid uniform so tightly her knuckles were stark white. Tears were
already spilling over her eyelashes, dropping onto her lap in heavy,
miserable splatters.

"I... I'm sorry..." Petra sobbed, her voice cracking as the guilt she
had been carrying finally broke through her enforced silence. "I was so
scared of the stories... I shouldn't have closed the window on you,
Emilia-sama... I'm so, so sorry..."

Frederica immediately wrapped an arm around the young girl, pulling her
close and letting Petra bury her face in her chest.

Emilia's eyes widened, and she quickly turned around in her seat to face
the weeping girl. "Petra, no, please don't cry!" Emilia pleaded, her own
shame forgotten in the face of the girl's distress. "You didn't do
anything wrong! I... I know I look scary. It's okay. You're here now,
and you're helping us so much. I don't hold it against you at all, I
promise!"

"She's far too forgiving," Ram muttered, though her eyes held a soft
glint as she watched the half-elf comfort her junior.

"*But we were the ones who turned you away." The elder looked at Emilia
with a cold expression. "If it's because you want us to back you in the
royal selection, that's just fine, so long as you come out and say so."*

*The elder woman's closed, ashen eyes were forced to open so she could
stare at Emilia clearly. As two leaders of their own factions. "But if
you have some other reason, we would like to know... that you are not a
witch who is beyond our comprehension."*

"Ah, the classic political shakedown," Anastasia hummed, leaning her
chin on her hand, her merchant instincts flaring to life. "The elder's a
smart cookie. She's giving Emilia-san an out, but also testing her. If
she lies here, they'll know. So, what's the play, I wonder?"

"A ruler must be resolute in such moments," Julius noted, his pen
pausing over his notebook. "If she shows weakness now, she will lose
whatever fragile trust she has begun to build."

*With this chance from the elder, Emilia noticed that the crowd had gone
silent and looked to her with curious gazes, open ones, even Subaru gave
her an encouraging expression with a nod to push her forward.*

Kenichi let out a soft breath, a proud smile pulling at the corner of
his lips. "There's my boy. Right in her corner."

"Always," Rem whispered from behind them. It was said with a mixture of
melancholy and profound respect. Rem knew better than anyone the power
of Subaru's unwavering belief.

*She turned from Subaru with a cool expression as she answered
truthfully.*

"*I don't have the confidence to give you a respectable answer right
now. I don't have any strong, convincing words that would satisfy all of
you. But... Though it wasn't for very long, I spent a few days with your
families, the ones who aren't here now. And it made me realize... that
families should be together."*

Anastasia clicked her tongue. "Oof. Startin' off by admittin' weakness?
That's a bold strategy. A bit too honest for the political stage, if ya
ask me."

"Listen to her, Anastasia-sama," Crusch interrupted softly, her amber
eyes fixed on the screen. "Sometimes, stripped of all grandiosity,
honesty is the sharpest blade one can wield."

Beatrice shifted on Subaru's lap, her butterfly pupils tracking Emilia's
hand on the screen. "... Bubby," the great spirit muttered softly, her
voice carrying a rare, vulnerable tone.

The Natsuki parents froze. The words struck them like a physical blow.

/Families should be together./

Naoko reached out blindly, her hand finding Kenichi's. He squeezed it
tightly, his jaw clenching as he looked from the screen down to the
sleeping face of his son. They were separated. Wrenched apart without
warning, without goodbyes. The raw, simple truth in Emilia's words
resonated so deeply within the Natsuki parents that it left an ache in
their chests.

*She unconsciously touched the green crystal on her chest, remembering
that she, too, was now separated from her relatives.*

"*I want to return all of you to your families. I didn't promise them
that I'd do so...But I promised myself. That's my only reason. I wasn't
really thinking about earning your support or anything. But if possible,
I'd like to... um...be friends with you all. That's what I want."*

Silence blanketed the theater. It wasn't a tense silence, but a warm,
heavy one.

"*Emilia-sama..." The elder addressed her.*

"*Y-Yes?" Emilia held her hands together, straightening her back infront
of the elder.*

"*I realize this will sound like we only care about what benefits us,
but... I pray you will accept our friendship." With these words, the
elder, followed by the rest of the residents, bowed their heads in front
of Emilia.*

"*P-Please, I should ask the same! I know I'm not worthy, but please
accept my friendship!" Emilia rattled excitedly and bowed again.*

*"I'm not worthy"? Who says that in this day and age?" Subaru gave a
relieved smile from behind her, hands on his hips in an all's well that
ends well vibe to celebrate the happy ending of Emilia's conversation
with the villagers.*

Emilia, sitting in her theater seat, let out a breath she hadn't
realized she was holding. A soft, watery smile bloomed on her face as
she watched the screen. To finally be accepted, not out of obligation or
fear, but out of genuine warmth... it was everything she had ever fought
for.

"Well I'll be," Felt grinned, crossing her arms. "She actually pulled it
off. Didn't even need a fancy speech."

"It lacked the eloquence of a seasoned diplomat," Wilhelm observed, a
rare, grandfatherly warmth in his eyes, "but it possessed the heart of a
true protector. Well done, Emilia-sama."

"Hmph. A display of naive sentimentality that only worked because of the
desperation of commoners," Priscilla fanned herself dismissively, though
her red eyes lingered on Emilia for a moment longer than usual. "Still,
one cannot argue with the results of the subjugation. They are yours
now, half-devil."

"It wasn't subjugation," Emilia protested weakly, though the sting of
Priscilla's words failed to penetrate her current relief.

Suddenly, a hand gently patted Emilia's shoulder.

She turned to see Naoko looking at her, tears shimmering in the older
woman's eyes. Naoko didn't say a word, she simply offered a bright,
watery smile that radiated absolute approval.

Beside her, Kenichi leaned over, giving Emilia a firm nod and a
thumbs-up.

"You know, Emilia-chan," Kenichi said, his voice a bit thicker than
usual. "Our boy can be a real idiot sometimes. Headstrong, reckless, and
way too loud." He looked down at Subaru, ruffling his black hair
affectionately before looking back up at the silver-haired girl. "But
seeing the kind of girl he's pouring his heart out for... seeing how
good your heart is? I think he's got the best taste in the whole
universe."

Emilia's face erupted in a brilliant shade of red. "I--- I'm--- Thank
you! I mean, I'm just---"

"He's right," Naoko interrupted softly, leaning closer. "Thank you,
Emilia-chan. For wanting families to be together."

Emilia looked between the two parents, the people who had lost their son
to her world. The weight of their gratitude, combined with the
acceptance on the screen, finally broke her composure. She hid her face
behind her hands, a few happy tears escaping her amethyst eyes as she
nodded fiercely.

*After leaving the building, he heard Ram's voice from behind him.*

"*Emilia-sama...seems to have changed a bit. Is it because of something
you said to her?" Ram crossed her arms and smiled warmly at him.*

In the theater, Rem blinked. She rubbed her eyes with the back of her
hands and stared at the screen as if it had suddenly grown a second
head. "Nee-sama is... smiling? At Subaru-kun? Without hiding an insult
in it?"

"I must be hallucinating," Otto whispered from his seat, leaning forward
so much he nearly fell out of his chair.

"The world's endin', bro," Garfiel muttered, his jaw practically hitting
the floor as he stared at the pink-haired maid on the screen. "Ram's
actually bein' nice to th' Cap'n."

In the back row, Ram stiffened. Her face flushed a very faint shade of
pink, and she defensively crossed her arms in an exact mirror of her
on-screen counterpart. "It is merely a trick of the lighting. Barusu is
incapable of drawing out a genuine smile from Ram."

"Oh? Is that so, my d~eee~ar Ram?" Roswaal chuckled beside her, his
mismatched eyes gleaming with immense amusement. "I suppose the 'trick
of the lighting' also altered the tone of your vo~iii~ce?"

Ram merely clicked her tongue and looked away, refusing to dignify her
master's teasing with a response.

"*It's the result of her thinking things through on her own." He replied
warmly. "You shouldn't assume all her decisions come from someone
else."*

Emilia lowered her hands from her face, the blush still dusting her
cheeks, but a profound sense of gratitude replaced her embarrassment.
Subaru wasn't taking credit for her growth. Even behind her back, he was
defending her autonomy and her strength.

"He's grown into a remarkably mature young man," Wilhelm observed
softly, nodding in quiet approval. "To guide someone, yet refuse to
claim ownership of their success... it is the mark of a true knight."

Kenichi puffed out his chest just a fraction more, beaming with fatherly
pride.

But it was the next exchange that truly shattered the theater's
composure.

"*You're right. I was in the wrong this time." Ram said guiltily.*

Dead silence reigned in the room for three entire seconds.

"HUH?!" Felt yelled, breaking the quiet. "Did the pink maid just
apologize to him?!"

"She admitted she was wrong!" Tivey gasped, adjusting his monocle as if
the glass had deceived him. "To Natsuki-san!"

"Nee-sama..." Rem whispered, looking back at her sister with an
expression of pure, unadulterated awe. "You actually apologized to
Subaru-kun..."

Ram's face was now burning a bright, undeniable red. She gripped her own
elbows tightly, glaring daggers at the back of everyone's heads. "Shut
your mouths, all of you! It was a momentary lapse in judgment! Barusu
clearly caught me when I was fatigued from dealing with his usual
nonsense!"

"Sure he did, Ram-san," Otto snickered, unable to help himself despite
the lethal glare he was receiving. "We completely believe you."

/*End credits: Memento*/

*The scene changed to sprouting fires, put on lamps to light up the
night sky. The grounds of the ruin that was at the center of the
Sanctuary were shown, with Emilia standing before it and a bunch of
glowing orbs lighting around her.*

"Woah... those are pretty." Kenichi commented with a look of awe. Naoko
kept his jaw closed by tapping his chin lightly.

"*Did you get enough support from the lesser spirits?" Subaru asked her,
Otto on the carriage beside him with Garfiel and Ram looking on from the
sidelines.*

"*Yeah. Everything's fine." She turns around with a small smile. "Still,
I think I'd like one last cheer."*

"Oho!" Kenichi grinned, leaning forward in his seat and rubbing his
hands together. "She's asking for it! Get ready, Naoko. He's about to do
the thing."

"The thing?" Crusch asked, arching an elegant eyebrow at the Natsuki
patriarch.

"You'll see," Naoko giggled behind her hand, her eyes twinkling with
amusement.

"*Can I do it?" Natsuki asked enthusiastically.*

"*I want you to. Please." Emilia gave him a knowing smile.*

Julius, who had been expecting a standard knightly vow of encouragement,
blinked in confusion at the sheer excitement radiating from Subaru. "He
looks as though he's been waiting his entire life for this specific
request."

He hadn't seen anything yet.

"*You can do it! Don't give up! EMC! Emilia-tan, major challenger!" He
shouted, bringing his all into it so his body could shape like the
letters in an amazing form of stupidity only available to the
autistic--- I mean artistic.*

Felt burst out laughing so hard she almost choked on her own spit.
"PFFT- AHAHAHA! What the hell is that?!"

"Such unorthodox poses..." Reinhard murmured, looking mildly impressed
despite himself. "I don't think I could contort my body with such speed
without utilizing my Divine Protection of Evasion."

"That ain't an evasion technique, Sword Saint, it's just plain stupid,"
Garfiel snorted, though he had a wide, toothy grin on his face. "Cap'n
sure knows how to make a fool of 'imself to make 'er smile."

Julius rubbed his temples, letting out a long, suffering sigh. "I take
back my previous statement about his maturity. The man has the dignity
of a court jester."

"Oh, hush, Julius-san," Anastasia giggled from her seat, thoroughly
entertained. "I think it's sweet! Ain't many men willin' to look that
foolish just to give their lady a boost."

"Truly, a display of ultimate foolishness, I suppose," Beatrice sighed,
crossing her arms over her chest, though there was an undeniable
fondness in her eyes. "Betty's Subaru is hopeless, in fact."

Kenichi, meanwhile, had thrown a fist into the air, mirroring his son's
energy. "That's my boy! You gotta put your whole back into it if you
want the girl to notice!"

"*Thank you." Emilia said, her smile bright and completely devoid of any
embarrassment at his antics.*

In the theater, Emilia hid the lower half of her face behind her silver
hair, though her amethyst eyes were crinkled in happiness. Even with an
audience judging him, his silly cheers still worked perfectly to warm
her heart.

*After that, Emilia headed for the entrance to the tomb, which began to
glow as she approached.*

"*That means she's been approved as a qualified challenger in the
Trial," Garfiel explained from the side, knowing Subaru was confused.*

"*I'm going now." The girl said confidently.*

"She looks so brave," Naoko whispered, squeezing her husband's hand. "I
hope it isn't too scary inside."

Emilia, in her seat, subconsciously pulled her knees closer together.
She knew exactly how terrifying the inside of that tomb was. The
memories of the snow, the blood, and the isolation were still too fresh
in her mind.

*Garfiel was looking at her retreating with a frown, which was noticed
by Subaru.*

"*I get that it sucks being trapped here." Subaru gained Garfiel's
attention as he hardened his eyes on him. "But why didn't you do this
yourself?"*

"*If I could, I would, damn it." The blond man grumbled with a
sharp-toothed snarl. Ram gave him a forlorn expression as Subaru raised
an eyebrow.*

In the theater, Garfiel clicked his tongue and sank lower into his seat,
crossing his arms defensively over his chest. He actively avoided making
eye contact with anyone, especially Frederica, who was looking at him
with a mixture of sorrow and understanding.

"It seems there is much more to Garfiel-dono's reluctance than simple
stubbornness," Wilhelm observed, his sharp eyes catching Ram's
uncharacteristic display of pity. "A past attempt, perhaps? One that
ended poorly?"

"Everyone in that Sanctuary's got a mountain of baggage," Al muttered
from the back, leaning his helmet against the wall.

*To their surprise, the glow of the tomb suddenly dried up.*

"*H-Hey, is she gonna be okay?" Subaru asked.*

"*The light's not supposed to go out as long as the trial's going on..."
Garfiel looked at the grounds with uncertainty.*

"What does that mean?!" Kenichi bolted upright in his chair, his
paternal worry instantly shifting from his son to the girl his son
loved. "Did something happen to her in there?"

"The trial was rejected?" Julius leaned forward, his brow furrowed in
deep concern. "Or perhaps an external force interfered?"

"Emilia-sama..." Rem whispered, looking toward the trembling half-elf.

Emilia sat completely frozen in the theater. She wrapped her arms around
herself as if staving off a sudden, biting chill. The lights going
out... she remembered exactly what happened next. The crushing weight of
her forgotten past, the paralyzing fear, and the agonizing feeling of
being utterly, completely alone in the dark.

"I..." Emilia forced the word out of her trembling lips, refusing to
look at the screen. "I failed... It was so cold..."

Seeing her distress, Naoko didn't hesitate. She leaned across the small
gap between their seats and gently placed a warm hand over Emilia's
shivering ones. "It's alright, sweetie," Naoko said softly, her motherly
tone cutting through the rising tension in the room. "You're right here.
You're safe."

Emilia let out a shaky breath, grounding herself in the warmth of
Naoko's touch, even as the screen promised to show everyone her darkest
moments.

"*Emilia!" Natsuki rushed into the tomb.*

"*Barusu!" Ram tried to stop him, worriedly.*

*To everyone's even greater surprise, the building began to glow again
as the young man approached.*

"*Why..." Ram said in disbelief.*

"*I don't understand, but if I can get in there, that's good enough for
me! Just stay there! I'll yell if I need you!" Subaru shouted at them
without stopping.*

"Always jumping headfirst into the fire," Julius sighed, though there
was an undeniable respect in his gaze. "A foolish knight, but a knight
nonetheless."

"So that's what Echidna gave to him..." Emilia pursed her lip, hugging
her knees tighter. "And he ran in after I had failed..."

*He ran along the glowing corridor until he saw an open door ahead. He
entered and found the lying unconscious Emilia.*

"*Emilia!"*

*But before he could reach her, his mind went blank, and a voice
suspiciously similar to his own sounded in his head.*

"/*First, you need to face your own past."*/

*After that, consciousness left him to fall right beside Emilia.*

**THUUUUUD **

The entire theater seemed to suck in a collective breath.

"His... past?" Kenichi breathed out, his voice suddenly hollow. He
slumped back into his seat, the jovial energy he had carried just
moments ago evaporating entirely.

Naoko's hand tightened around Kenichi's arm in a vise grip. Her eyes
were wide, glued to the screen in a mixture of profound hope and
paralyzing fear. "Are we... are we going to see..." She couldn't finish
the sentence.

"His home," Al whispered from the back, his helmet shifting slightly as
he leaned forward, entirely captivated. The single word hung in the air,
heavy with the weight of another world.

Emilia and Rem both stared at the screen, their hearts pounding against
their ribs. They were finally going to see it. The world that shaped
him. The life he had lived before he became their hero, before the pain,
before the looping. The boy named Natsuki Subaru, before Lugunica.

*He woke up in bed... In a strange room darkened by curtains. There were
various books on the shelves above his bed with small figurines of
little characters in colorful, stylish outfits, frozen in time, doing
complex poses to show off their figures.*

"*Good morning, son of mine!" The next moment, a muscular middle-aged
man with short black hair, naked, well-trained torso, and in orange
sweatpants fell on the Subaru.*

*Subaru groaned as the weight of the man settled onto him, all sleep
gone from his eyes as his lungs battled for air.*

"*Hey, c'mon! What? What? What's wrong? You look as if you just saw your
own dad naked first thing in the morning!" The newcomer stood tall,
smiling down at Subaru with a shine on his teeth that resembled
Garfiel's and Subaru's own throughout the entire viewing.*

*Subaru looked at him... dumbfounded.*

/*Title card: The Long-Awaited Reunion*/

/*Season 2 Episode 3. End.*/

In the theater, the shock was instantaneous and absolutely deafening.

Nearly every single head in the room snapped so fast they risked
whiplash, looking from the muscular, grinning man on the screen directly
to the man sitting in the middle row holding his wife's hand.

Kenichi Natsuki sat utterly paralyzed. His mouth opened, but no sound
came out. The jovial, confident patriarch of the Natsuki household was
reduced to a trembling statue as he stared at his own face projected on
the giant screen.

"Wait... wait a second!" Garfiel yelled, pointing a shaking finger at
the screen and then at Kenichi. "That's... That's you! Th' exact same
teeth 'n everythin'!"

"Natsuki-san?" Otto gasped, completely bewildered by the sudden
appearance of the man sitting right next to him.

"It's... it's really him," Emilia whispered, her amethyst eyes darting
between the screen and Kenichi. The realization hit her like a tidal
wave. "Subaru... He... The same thing as my trial..."

"Oh, my baby..." Naoko finally broke. The dam she had been holding back
since the very beginning of this nightmare shattered into a million
pieces. She buried her face into Kenichi's shoulder, her shoulders
heaving with violent, agonizing sobs. "He's home... look, Kenichi, he's
in his bed. He's safe."

Kenichi's eyes immediately filled with heavy, unshed tears. He wrapped
both arms around Naoko, burying his face in her hair as his own chest
hitched uncontrollably. The sight of that normal, mundane morning---a
morning he had taken for granted a thousand times over, a routine he
thought would last forever---was a dagger straight to his heart.

It was the morning his son had disappeared. The last morning they had
ever spent together.

"Yeah... yeah, he is," Kenichi choked out, his voice thick with a grief
so profound it silenced the rest of the room. He looked up at the
screen, tears freely streaming down his cheeks as he watched his
on-screen self smile down at his boy.

"That's my boy," Kenichi wept, pulling his wife closer as the entire
theater watched the Natsuki family reunite, if only in a memory. "That's
my stupid, wonderful boy."

Rem covered her mouth with both hands, her own tears falling freely as
she watched the Natsuki parents break down. She looked at the screen, at
the dumbfounded Subaru, and then at the father who loved him so
fiercely.

"Told ya..." Felt couldn't but grimace at the parents from her seat.
"This will only get worse from 'ere."

"This prison and it's cruelty..." Crusch looked away from the pair so
she can give them a little bit of respect as the screen above refused to
let them rest.

"Sooner or later... You'll be able to talk with him all about it,
Natsuki-san." Otto whispered over the two, as they held each other
beside him. Having Garfiel nod his head firmly.

"As soon as he wakes up... I promise."

Roswaal's eccentric smile turned thin after hearing Otto's words. With
his fingers intertwining.

/*Next up, Episode 3... Arc 4's continuation...\\
*/

enjoyed it. It's one of my funniest if I could say so myself. I enjoyed
toying with so many dynamics at once.*
